SETDB1は進化的に若いレトロエレメントを優先的にサイレンシングする。
トランスポゾン（TE）は、ヘテロクロマチン形成やDNAメチル化など、複数のメカニズムによってエピジェネティックにサイレンシングされる。これまでの研究では、SETDB1とヒトサイレンシングハブ（HUSH）複合体がヘテロクロマチン形成に基づくTE抑制に関与していることが示唆されてきたが、SETDB1および関連メカニズムにおける反復配列サブクラスおよび遺伝子座特異的な効果の精密な解析は、ショートリードのトランスクリプトームマッピングによって制限されてきた。本研究では、テロメアからテロメアまでのマウスゲノムアセンブリにマッピングされたロングリードRNAシーケンスを用いて、単一遺伝子座レベルでのTEサイレンシングにおけるSETDB1の特異的な寄与を解明した。SETDB1またはHUSHコア因子のノックダウンにより、全ゲノムDNA脱メチル化とは異なる、限られたサブセットの長末端反復配列（LTR）レトロエレメントの強力かつ再現性のある脱抑制が明らかになった。この脱抑制はTEサブクラス内で不均一であり、個別のゲノム領域に限定されているため、集約的なファミリーレベルの解析では隠蔽される制御の多様性が強調されます。時間分解SETDB1枯渇に続いてヒストン3リジン9（H3K9）me3、H3K9me2、およびPol II ChIP-seqを行うと、階層化された脱抑制応答が明らかになります。ERVKおよびERV1要素は、H3K9メチル化の喪失、Pol IIの増加、およびRNA発現の誘導が一致して示され、H3K9me3を介した直接的なサイレンシングと一致しますが、LINE/L1要素は対応するH3K9me3の喪失なしに転写活性化を示し、並行した抑制メカニズムが関与していることを示唆しています。これらのデータは、SETDB1依存性H3K9メチル化が進化的に若いLTRのサイレンシングを維持する一方で、長鎖散在反復配列（LINE）による抑制は、単一のクロマチン解析では完全に捉えきれない、より広範なヘテロクロマチン経路に関与するというモデルを支持する。総合的に見て、我々の知見は、ヘテロクロマチンを介したTE制御に関する既存のモデルを改良し、クロマチンに基づくメカニズムがどのように協調してサブクラス選択的なTE抑制を維持しているかを解明するための枠組みを提供する。

Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A · IF 9.4 · 2026-10-08
Transcriptional regulation and function of GPR18 in macrophage subtype specification.
Mena HA, Patsalos A, Altomare J, …, Spite M
▾

Abstract

Resolution of inflammation is mediated in part by agonists that engage immune cells to dampen inflammatory signaling, promote phagocytic functions, and facilitate tissue repair. G protein-coupled receptor 18 (GPR18) has emerged as an important proresolving receptor that is activated by endogenous mediators including Resolvin D2. However, very little is known about how GPR18 expression is controlled in immune cells, and its cell-autonomous roles during resolution of inflammation remain of interest. Here, we elucidated transcriptional and epigenetic regulation of Gpr18 in macrophages (MFs). We identified binding of monocyte/macrophage lineage-determining transcription factors (LDTFs) including PU.1, IRF8, and RUNX1 at the Gpr18 locus in the basal state in murine bone marrow-derived macrophages (BMDM). We found that Gpr18 is highly induced in BMDM by inflammatory stimuli including interferon gamma (IFNγ) and ligands for multiple toll-like receptors. Motif analysis predicted enrichment for LDTFs and signal-dependent TFs including nuclear factor κB (NFκB) and interferon regulatory factors (IRF) at the Gpr18 locus. Stimulation of BMDM with lipopolysaccharide (LPS) or IFNγ increased the binding of NFκB p65 and IRF1 at the Gpr18 locus, which played a causal role in the induction of Gpr18 by LPS or IFNγ, respectively. Mice with myeloid deficiency of Gpr18 had increased tissue swelling and accumulation of "inflammatory" Ly6Chi monocytes/MFs during contact hypersensitivity, which was recapitulated during acute peritonitis and led to altered transcriptomes of resolution-phase MFs. These results demonstrate that inflammatory signaling primes MFs for resolution in part by inducing Gpr18, which plays a cell-autonomous role to regulate MF phenotypic transitions.

マクロファージサブタイプ分化におけるGPR18の転写調節と機能
炎症の解消は、免疫細胞を活性化して炎症シグナル伝達を抑制し、貪食機能を促進し、組織修復を促進するアゴニストによって部分的に媒介されます。Gタンパク質共役型受容体18（GPR18）は、レゾルビンD2などの内因性メディエーターによって活性化される重要な炎症解消促進受容体として注目されています。しかし、免疫細胞におけるGPR18の発現制御機構についてはほとんど解明されておらず、炎症解消における細胞自律的な役割は依然として関心を集めています。本研究では、マクロファージ（MF）におけるGPR18の転写およびエピジェネティックな調節機構を明らかにしました。マウス骨髄由来マクロファージ（BMDM）の基礎状態において、PU.1、IRF8、RUNX1などの単球/マクロファージ系統決定転写因子（LDTF）がGPR18遺伝子座に結合することを確認しました。我々は、Gpr18がインターフェロンγ（IFNγ）や複数のトール様受容体のリガンドを含む炎症性刺激によって骨髄由来マクロファージ（BMDM）で高度に誘導されることを発見した。モチーフ解析により、Gpr18遺伝子座にはLDTFおよび核因子κB（NFκB）やインターフェロン調節因子（IRF）などのシグナル依存性転写因子が濃縮されていることが予測された。リポ多糖（LPS）またはIFNγによるBMDMの刺激は、Gpr18遺伝子座におけるNFκB p65およびIRF1の結合を増加させ、それぞれLPSまたはIFNγによるGpr18誘導に因果関係のある役割を果たした。Gpr18の骨髄系欠損マウスでは、接触過敏症時に組織の腫脹と「炎症性」Ly6Chi単球/MFの蓄積が増加し、これは急性腹膜炎時に再現され、回復期MFのトランスクリプトームの変化につながった。これらの結果は、炎症シグナルがGpr18を誘導することによって部分的にMFの解消を準備し、Gpr18はMFの表現型変化を制御する細胞自律的な役割を果たすことを示している。

Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A · IF 9.4 · 2026-10-06
53BP1 condensates regulate DNA replication progression and completion.
Wang F, Geng X, Zhang J, …, Zhang Y
▾

Abstract

DNA replication in mammalian cells proceeds in chromosomal domains during defined windows of S phase; however, the mechanisms coordinating replication with restoration of chromatin states remain incompletely understood. Here, we identify p53-binding protein 1 (53BP1) condensates as regulators of heterochromatin replication and epigenetic restoration. Loss of 53BP1 delays mid-to-late S-phase progression, compromises recruitment of epigenetic restoration factors, and impairs timely completion of heterochromatin replication and restoration of heterochromatin-associated epigenetic marks. These defects were rescued by wild-type or condensate-competent 53BP1, but not by condensate-deficient mutants. Further, 53BP1 condensate formation and its role in heterochromatin replication depend on interaction with proliferating cell nuclear antigen, which also mediates 53BP1 relocalization from condensates to DNA damage foci upon genotoxic stress. Together, our findings reveal a previously unrecognized role for 53BP1 condensates in coordinating heterochromatin replication and epigenetic restoration, while serving as a reservoir for DNA damage response.

哺乳類細胞におけるDNA複製は、S期の特定の期間に染色体領域内で進行する。しかし、複製とクロマチン状態の回復を協調させるメカニズムは、いまだ十分に解明されていない。本研究では、p53結合タンパク質1（53BP1）凝縮体がヘテロクロマチン複製とエピジェネティック修復の制御因子であることを明らかにした。53BP1の欠損は、S期中期から後期への進行を遅延させ、エピジェネティック修復因子のリクルートを阻害し、ヘテロクロマチン複製の適時完了とヘテロクロマチン関連エピジェネティックマークの修復を阻害する。これらの欠陥は、野生型または凝縮体形成能を有する53BP1によって回復したが、凝縮体欠損変異体では回復しなかった。さらに、53BP1凝縮体の形成とそのヘテロクロマチン複製における役割は、増殖細胞核抗原との相互作用に依存しており、この抗原は遺伝毒性ストレス時に53BP1を凝縮体からDNA損傷部位へ再局在化させる役割も担っている。これらの知見を総合すると、53BP1凝縮体はヘテロクロマチン複製とエピジェネティック修復の調整において、これまで認識されていなかった役割を果たし、同時にDNA損傷応答の貯蔵庫としても機能することが明らかになった。

Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A · IF 9.4 · 2026-10-06
Phenotypic screening identifies kenpaullone as a prolymphangiogenic compound to improve heart repair following myocardial infarction.
Ravaud C, Cuoco CA, Sigal S, …, Riley PR
▾

Abstract

Myocardial infarction (MI) induces cardiac muscle death and its subsequent replacement by a noncontractile fibrotic scar, which underlies progression to heart failure. Current treatments restore blood flow and assist with cardiac workload but do not promote regeneration. We have previously shown endogenous growth of the cardiac lymphatic system following MI and further stimulation with the lymphatic endothelial specific mutated Vascular Endothelial Growth Factor-C (VEGF-C) isoform, VEGFC-C156S, resolves the immune response and improves cardiac function. However, the short half-life of VEGFC-C156S makes it suboptimal for clinical use, highlighting the need for alternative prolymphangiogenic strategies. We established a spheroid-based sprouting assay using human lymphatic endothelial cells to mimic lymphangiogenesis and performed a phenotypic screen of focused libraries of epigenetic regulators, kinase inhibitors, and stem cell modulators. Automated imaging and quantitative analysis identified several kinase inhibitors, previously characterized as GSK3ß inhibitors, as prolymphangiogenic regulators. Among them, the most potent was kenpaullone, which activated the same canonical prolymphangiogenic ERK pathway as VEGF-C, but induced a distinct transcriptional response in treated spheroids. Using kenpaullone-derived chemical probes and target-validation approaches, we identified MAP4K4 as a molecular target mediating the prolymphangiogenic effect observed. Finally, in a mouse MI model, kenpaullone enhanced cardiac lymphangiogenesis and improved cardiac function.

心筋梗塞（MI）は心筋細胞の壊死とその後の非収縮性線維性瘢痕組織への置換を引き起こし、これが心不全への進行の根底にあります。現在の治療法は血流を回復させ、心臓の負荷を軽減しますが、再生を促進するものではありません。私たちは以前、MI後に心臓リンパ系の内因性増殖が起こること、そしてリンパ管内皮細胞特異的変異型血管内皮増殖因子C（VEGF-C）アイソフォームであるVEGFC-C156Sによる刺激が免疫応答を抑制し、心機能を改善することを示しました。しかし、VEGFC-C156Sの半減期が短いため、臨床使用には最適とは言えず、代替となるリンパ管新生促進戦略の必要性が浮き彫りになっています。我々は、リンパ管新生を模倣するためにヒトリンパ管内皮細胞を用いたスフェロイドベースの発芽アッセイを確立し、エピジェネティック制御因子、キナーゼ阻害剤、および幹細胞調節因子のフォーカスライブラリーの表現型スクリーニングを実施した。自動画像解析と定量的解析により、これまでGSK3β阻害剤として特徴づけられていたいくつかのキナーゼ阻害剤が、リンパ管新生促進因子として同定された。その中で最も強力であったのはケンパウロンであり、VEGF-Cと同じ典型的なリンパ管新生促進ERK経路を活性化したが、処理したスフェロイドでは異なる転写応答を誘導した。ケンパウロン由来の化学プローブと標的検証アプローチを用いて、観察されたリンパ管新生促進効果を媒介する分子標的としてMAP4K4を同定した。最後に、マウス心筋梗塞モデルにおいて、ケンパウロンは心臓リンパ管新生を促進し、心機能を改善した。

Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A · IF 9.4 · 2026-10-05
Chronic cigarette smoke exposure induces distinct stem cell states driving genetic driver-specific non-small cell lung cancer subtypes.
Wang N, Padaki R, Thursby SJ, …, Vaz M
▾

Abstract

Cigarette smoke-induced chronic inflammation (CI) potentiates development of non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) by mediating genetic and epigenetic events. Using normal lung organoids (LOs), we define how chronic cigarette smoke condensate (CSC) exposure drives two separate stem cell populations to evolve premalignant states harboring progressive epigenetic and linked transcriptomic abnormalities in the absence of major driver mutations. These dynamics facilitate evolution of an immune evasive state with downregulation of inflammatory pathways and accompanying death signals mediated by epigenetic silencing of PANoptosis regulator, Zbp1. The stem cell populations evolve through distinct trajectories to respond differently to subsequent introduction of oncogenic mutations, KrasG12V and loss of Tp53, to induce one step transformation of CSC exposed organoids resulting in two major NSCLC subtypes. Kras mutations drive tumorigenesis primarily in a bronchioalveolar stem cell-derived state producing adenocarcinomas while TP53 loss drives tumorigenesis in a basal stem cell-derived state resulting in squamous cell carcinomas. CSC-induced downregulation of Zbp1 and interferon signaling is further potentiated in tumors with mutation-specific changes marked by decreased expression in Kras-mutant contexts. Our findings define unique transcriptomic profiles in CI-induced stem cell states and reveal a key role for cell death pathway-associated genes in potentiating oncogenic drivers to promote NSCLC. Together, our data suggest strategies to predict cancer risk and enable early interception.

慢性的なタバコ煙曝露は、遺伝子ドライバー特異的な非小細胞肺癌サブタイプを駆動する、異なる幹細胞状態を誘導する。

Epigenetics (journals)epigen*PubMedDOI
Wang N, Padaki R, Thursby SJ, Yen RC, Cope LM, Brock MV, Gabrielson E, Easwaran H, Baylin SB, Vaz M[Journal Article]
2
Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A · IF 9.4 · 2026-11-03
Rising cell-free DNA level tracks cancer-associated methylation signal and improves multicancer detection.
Basu S, Hiremath P, Rathod N, …, Cantor CR
▾

Abstract

Cancer patients carry more circulating cell-free DNA (ccfDNA)-small DNA fragments shed into the blood-than healthy people. Most of this excess comes not from the tumor itself but from immune cells, suggesting that it would simply dilute the much smaller amount of tumor-derived DNA in a blood sample. Using a study of 1,480 previously untreated participants across 43 hospitals-including patients with 14 cancer types spanning all stages, patients with benign growths, and healthy volunteers-we show the opposite: as total ccfDNA rises, it becomes enriched, not diluted, in fragments carrying cancer-associated DNA methylation signatures, independent of cancer type, disease stage, and a broad panel of demographic, clinical, and technical covariates. In fact, total ccfDNA explains more variance in cancer-associated methylation signal than the disease stage itself. This basic biological finding suggests a coupling between immune cell activity and tumor methylation. It also has a practical implication: we show that rising ccfDNA concentration is associated with higher cancer detection sensitivity, even within disease stages and particularly in early-stage disease-stage I sensitivity varied from 0.55 in the lowest ccfDNA concentration tertile to 0.85 in the highest, and stage II from 0.59 to 0.86. This suggests that ccfDNA concentration should be considered as a stage-independent stratification variable for benchmarking cancer-detection tests. Interestingly, similar behavior was observed in patients with symptomatic benign lesions, suggesting a broader biological response to abnormal tissue growth and not something unique to malignancy.

日本語訳（機械翻訳）

細胞遊離DNAレベルの上昇は、がん関連メチル化シグナルを追跡し、多発がんの検出精度を向上させる。
がん患者は、健常者よりも多くの循環細胞遊離DNA（ccfDNA）――血液中に放出される小さなDNA断片――を保有している。この過剰なccfDNAの大部分は腫瘍自体ではなく免疫細胞由来であり、血液サンプル中のごく少量の腫瘍由来DNAを希釈するだけだと考えられていた。しかし、43の病院で治療を受けていない1,480人の参加者（あらゆる病期にわたる14種類のがん患者、良性腫瘍患者、健常ボランティアを含む）を対象とした研究により、我々は正反対の結果を示した。総ccfDNA量が増加するにつれて、がん関連DNAメチル化シグネチャーを持つ断片が希釈されるのではなく、濃縮されることが明らかになった。これは、がんの種類、病期、および広範な人口統計学的、臨床的、技術的な共変量とは無関係であった。実際、総ccfDNA量は、病期そのものよりもがん関連メチル化シグナルの変動をより多く説明する。この基本的な生物学的知見は、免疫細胞の活性と腫瘍のメチル化との間に何らかの関連性があることを示唆している。また、実用的な意味合いもあります。cfDNA濃度の上昇は、疾患の病期内、特に早期の病期において、がん検出感度の向上と関連していることが示されました。ステージIの感度は、cfDNA濃度が最も低い三分位群で0.55から最も高い三分位群で0.85まで変化し、ステージIIでは0.59から0.86まで変化しました。これは、cfDNA濃度ががん検出検査のベンチマークのための病期に依存しない層別化変数として考慮されるべきであることを示唆しています。興味深いことに、同様の挙動は症状のある良性病変の患者でも観察され、これは異常な組織増殖に対するより広範な生物学的反応であり、悪性腫瘍に特有のものではないことを示唆しています。

EMBO J · IF 9.4 · 2026-10-17
Electrostatic control of chromatin compaction safeguards against apoptotic DNA release.
Spicer MFD, Wijma S, Schütte N, …, Gerlich DW
▾

Abstract

Apoptosis involves extensive intracellular reorganisation to facilitate the clearance of dying cells. A key step in this process is the destruction of the genome through fragmentation by caspase-activated DNase (CAD). Rather than dispersing after CAD-mediated cleavage, DNA fragments are compacted into a dense chromatin compartment, but the underlying mechanism and biological relevance of this compaction remain unknown. Here we show that global deacetylation of histone tails promotes chromatin compaction during apoptosis, preventing DNA release into apoptotic extracellular vesicles. Using synthetic effectors that modulate nucleosome electrostatics independently of histone modifications, we demonstrate that electrostatic attraction alone is sufficient to compact and sequester fragmented chromatin. These findings reveal a mechanism by which global reprogramming of histone modifications coordinates genome fragmentation with its physical sequestration during apoptosis. Our synthetic approach further provides a tool to probe how physical forces contribute to genome organisation across diverse biological contexts.

クロマチン凝縮の静電制御は、アポトーシスにおけるDNA放出を防ぐ。
アポトーシスは、死滅細胞の除去を促進するために、細胞内で大規模な再編成を伴う。この過程における重要なステップは、カスパーゼ活性化DNase（CAD）による断片化を介したゲノムの破壊である。CADによる切断後、DNA断片は分散するのではなく、高密度のクロマチンコンパートメントに凝縮されるが、この凝縮の根底にあるメカニズムと生物学的意義は不明であった。本研究では、ヒストン尾部の全体的な脱アセチル化がアポトーシス中のクロマチン凝縮を促進し、アポトーシス性細胞外小胞へのDNA放出を防ぐことを示す。ヒストン修飾とは独立してヌクレオソームの静電特性を調節する合成エフェクターを用いることで、静電引力のみで断片化したクロマチンを凝縮・隔離できることを明らかにした。これらの知見は、ヒストン修飾の全体的な再プログラミングが、アポトーシス中のゲノム断片化とその物理的隔離を協調させるメカニズムを明らかにするものである。さらに、我々の合成的なアプローチは、多様な生物学的状況において、物理的な力がゲノム構造にどのように寄与しているかを解明するためのツールを提供する。

Cell Rep · IF 7.5 · 2026-10-28
Sleep-dependent DNA methylation remodeling in basolateral amygdala fear engrams.
Chen X, Ruan F, Rodriguez AM, …, Liu XS
▾

Abstract

How sleep consolidates fear memories at the molecular level within individual engram neurons is unknown. Using activity-dependent tagging and sequencing, we profiled the DNA methylome and transcriptome of engram and non-engram nuclei from the basolateral amygdala (BLA) after contextual fear conditioning with and without post-learning sleep deprivation. Fear learning induces coordinated CpG and CpH DNA methylation remodeling specifically in engram neurons, with each showing unique temporal dynamics, revealing CpH methylation as a separate component of the engram's epigenetic signature. Interestingly, sleep deprivation partially reverses learning-induced methylation changes and impairs memory recall, indicating that sleep supports engram-specific epigenetic consolidation. Integrating the engram methylome with single-cell transcriptome profiling, we construct a multi-omic molecular atlas of the BLA fear engram and identify Mid1 as a learning- and sleep-regulated methylation target. BLA-wide and engram-specific Mid1 knockdown establishes its functional requirement for fear memory consolidation, providing an approach to identify behaviorally relevant epigenetic targets.

Cell Rep · IF 7.5 · 2026-10-23
RIF1 coordinates the stepwise post-replicative H3K9me3 recovery.
Chen N, Bandau S, Nair RR, …, Buonomo SCB
▾

Abstract

DNA replication dilutes parental histones and their post-translational modifications, requiring restoration of epigenetic marks on newly incorporated histones. H3K9me3 restoration is unusually slow, extending into the following G1 phase, but the mechanisms governing this delay remain unclear. Here, we show that RIF1 reassociation with heterochromatin during mitotic exit establishes a permissive chromatin environment for histone methyltransferases to resume H3K9me3 deposition. RIF1 promotes recruitment of SUV39H1 and HP1α/β and the increase of H3K9 tri-methylation during G1. RIF1 also recruits protein phosphatase 1α (PP1α) to heterochromatin, and RIF1-PP1α interaction is essential for the maintenance of H3K9me3 levels. Moreover, RIF1-PP1 complex temporally restrains the activity of Aurora kinase at heterochromatin, preventing premature H3S10 phosphorylation before replication and creating a permissive time-window for SUV39H-mediated reinstatement of H3K9me3.

RIF1は、複製後の段階的なH3K9me3回復を調節する。
DNA複製は親ヒストンとその翻訳後修飾を希釈するため、新たに取り込まれたヒストン上のエピジェネティックマークの回復が必要となる。H3K9me3の回復は異常に遅く、次のG1期まで続くが、この遅延を制御するメカニズムは不明である。本研究では、有糸分裂終期におけるRIF1のヘテロクロマチンへの再結合が、ヒストンメチルトランスフェラーゼによるH3K9me3沈着再開に適したクロマチン環境を確立することを示す。RIF1は、G1期におけるSUV39H1およびHP1α/βのリクルートメントとH3K9トリメチル化の増加を促進する。RIF1はまた、プロテインホスファターゼ1α（PP1α）をヘテロクロマチンにリクルートし、RIF1-PP1α相互作用はH3K9me3レベルの維持に不可欠である。さらに、RIF1-PP1複合体はヘテロクロマチンにおけるオーロラキナーゼの活性を一時的に抑制し、複製前のH3S10の時期尚早なリン酸化を防ぎ、SUV39Hを介したH3K9me3の回復のための許容的な時間的窓を作り出す。

Cell Rep · IF 7.5 · 2026-10-06
A near-complete quail genome identifies heterochromatin expansion and chromosome architecture remodeling.
Liu J, Guo Q, Fan S, …, Chang G
▾

Abstract

Avian genomes are typically compact, with limited repetitive content, but the Japanese quail (Coturnix japonica) represents an exception. Here, we report a near-complete chromosome-level quail genome assembly (1.33 Gb), up to 43% larger than previous quail assemblies. The assembly reveals extensive repeat accumulation and heterochromatin expansion, particularly in the pericentromeric regions of micro- and dot chromosomes, and identifies a conserved centromeric repeat shared across chromosomes. Comparative analyses show that transposable element accumulation reshaped chromatin architecture, histone modification landscapes, and interchromosomal interactions. The female-specific W chromosome underwent extensive heterochromatinization and gene degeneration. Integrated epigenomic profiling further identifies quail-specific chromatin states. These findings reveal quail-specific heterochromatin expansion and provide new insights into avian centromere evolution, heterochromatin biology, and sex chromosome remodeling.

Cell Rep · IF 7.5 · 2026-10-23
Embryonic reprogramming drives anaplastic transformation in thyroid carcinoma.
Kawaura R, Unome A, Tomita H, …, Shibata H
▾

Abstract

Anaplastic thyroid carcinoma (ATC) is a highly aggressive malignancy that often arises through anaplastic transformation of papillary thyroid carcinoma (PTC). Although genetic drivers such as BRAF and TP53 mutations are implicated, the mechanisms underlying this transition remain poorly understood. Here, we show that human ATC loses thyroid differentiation and acquires pluripotency-associated features, accompanied by MAPK pathway activation. Guided by parallels with induced pluripotent stem cell reprogramming, we develop an in vivo reprogramming mouse model. Forced expression of Oct3/4, Sox2, Klf4, and c-Myc (OSKM) induces anaplastic transformation of Braf-mutant thyroid epithelium even without Trp53 mutation, yielding an ATC-like phenotype that shares molecular and histological features with human ATC. A conventional Braf/Trp53-mutant model shows similar features. Reintroduction of NKX2-1 into human and murine ATC cells partially reverses embryonic reprogramming, suppresses proliferation and induces redifferentiation toward a PTC-like phenotype. Embryonic reprogramming is a central driver of anaplastic transformation and supports redifferentiation strategies.

胚発生期の再プログラミングが甲状腺癌の未分化形質転換を促進する。

Cell Rep · IF 7.5 · 2026-10-18
Sequential TPRX1 and HERVL activation delineates a progression toward the totipotent-like state.
Huang A, Jiang N, Luo S, …, Xie B
▾

Abstract

The in vitro induction of 8-cell-like cells (8CLCs) from human pluripotent stem cells (hPSCs) provides a model to study the regulatory principles of totipotency and zygotic genome activation (ZGA). However, the cellular dynamics underlying 8CLC emergence remain poorly understood. Here, we generate a TPRX1-EGFP/HERVL-tdTomato dual-reporter hPSC line to resolve the stepwise acquisition of totipotent-like identity. We show that 8CLC induction follows an ordered trajectory in which TPRX1 activation precedes the emergence of TPRX1+HERVL+ cells, which most closely resemble the in vivo 8-cell embryo transcriptionally. Single-cell transcriptomics and time-resolved flow cytometry further define a continuous transition from the primed state through a TPRX1+ intermediate to the HERVL+ 8CLC state. CRISPRi and RNA-directed perturbations support functional contributions of HERVL elements and HERVL-derived RNA to this transition, while potential locus-dependent mechanisms remain unresolved. Together, these findings establish sequential TPRX1 and HERVL activation as a defining feature of human 8CLC induction.

Cell Rep · IF 7.5 · 2026-10-15
Genome architecture shapes transcriptional responses to topoisomerase depletion in C. elegans.
Morao AK, Chervova A, Kotte A, …, Cecere G
▾

Abstract

DNA supercoiling is an intrinsic consequence of transcription that must be resolved to maintain proper gene expression. How supercoiling shapes transcription dynamics in animal genomes remains unclear. Here, we acutely depleted topoisomerases in Caenorhabditis elegans and applied nascent transcription profiling, nuclear RNA sequencing (RNA-seq), histone modification mapping, and long-read sequencing to capture the transcriptional and chromatin responses to topological stress. We find that topoisomerase depletion preferentially inhibits and uncouples expression of convergent genes, while transcription initiation and coordinated expression of divergent genes are comparatively maintained. Topoisomerase degradation hinders transcription elongation globally, resulting in reduced production of longer transcripts and overall shortening of poly(A) tails. These elongation defects are not accompanied by global changes in elongation-associated histone modifications but coincide with modest reductions in promoter and enhancer marks. Our results indicate that genome architecture shapes transcriptional responses to DNA supercoiling, uncovering a topological basis for coordinated gene expression in a multicellular organism.

日本語訳（機械翻訳）

ゲノム構造は、線虫C. elegansにおけるトポイソメラーゼ欠損に対する転写応答を左右する。
DNAの超らせん構造は転写の必然的な結果であり、適切な遺伝子発現を維持するためには解消されなければならない。動物ゲノムにおける転写ダイナミクスを超らせん構造がどのように制御するのかは、依然として不明である。本研究では、線虫Caenorhabditis elegansにおいてトポイソメラーゼを急性的に欠損させ、新生転写プロファイリング、核RNAシーケンス（RNA-seq）、ヒストン修飾マッピング、およびロングリードシーケンスを用いて、トポロジー的ストレスに対する転写およびクロマチン応答を解析した。その結果、トポイソメラーゼ欠損は収束遺伝子の発現を優先的に阻害し、その発現を阻害する一方、発散遺伝子の転写開始と協調的発現は比較的維持されることが明らかになった。トポイソメラーゼの分解は転写伸長を全体的に阻害し、より長い転写産物の産生減少とポリ(A)テールの全体的な短縮をもたらす。これらの伸長異常は、伸長関連ヒストン修飾の全体的な変化を伴わないが、プロモーターおよびエンハンサーのマークのわずかな減少と一致する。我々の結果は、ゲノム構造がDNA超らせん化に対する転写応答を形成し、多細胞生物における協調的な遺伝子発現のトポロジー的基盤を明らかにすることを示している。

Cell Rep · IF 7.5 · 2026-10-15
Histone H3K27 methylation states are sequentially catalyzed in cycling cells.
Greene JE, Ahmad K, Henikoff S
▾

Abstract

Polycomb domains form at silenced genes and are marked by histone H3 lysine 27 tri-methylation, but it is unclear how this methylation is maintained following DNA replication in proliferating cells. Here, we use CUT&Tag chromatin profiling to track the dynamics of H3K27 methylation in human cells. We find that tri-methylation in Polycomb domains is maintained by stepwise methylation after DNA replication. Outside of Polycomb domains, thousands of transcriptionally inactive genes gain histone H3K27 di-methylation hours after DNA replication. Acute treatment with small-molecule inhibitors of the Polycomb repressive complex 2 (PRC2) histone methyltransferase slows H3K27 methylation during S-phase of the cell cycle and increases acetylation of the residue. This effect is most pronounced at Polycomb domains replicating in early S-phase, thereby suggesting that early replication and slow histone methylation confer plasticity to developmentally silenced genes. Together, our results explain how H3K27 modification patterns are faithfully duplicated in rapidly proliferating cells.

Cell Rep · IF 7.5 · 2026-10-13
Molecular mechanisms of promoter silencing by transcriptional readthrough.
Shipman GA, Walback E, González AG, …, Majewski J
▾

Abstract

Transcriptional interference is the in cis suppression of one transcriptional process by another. To define the mechanisms by which transcriptional readthrough is converted into stable gene silencing, we develop a mouse cell line model of cobalamin C deficiency associated with MMACHC silencing (epi-cblC) by deleting the Prdx1 intron 5 splice acceptor, thereby inducing antisense transcription across Mmachc. Readthrough results in ectopic H3K36me3 deposition, de novo DNA methylation, and stable silencing of Mmachc. Through systematic perturbations of transcription and the H3K36 and DNA methylation machinery, we demonstrate that antisense transcription is the primary driver of repression, whereas H3K36me3 contributes directly to promoter silencing and is further required for the establishment of DNA methylation, which in turn confers long-term stability. Together, these findings define a hierarchical silencing cascade by which transcriptional readthrough establishes stable gene repression and demonstrate that transcription itself can direct local chromatin remodeling and changes in gene expression.

Abstract

RUNX1 coordinates phenotypic gene expression, and its suppression or perturbations are associated with breast tumor initiation and progression. Mechanisms governing the dual roles of RUNX1 in sustaining the mammary epithelial phenotype while suppressing initiation of cancer-compromised gene expression are unclear. Abstract

Symbiont-derived metabolites can regulate host physiology, but how they affect xenobiotic detoxification remains poorly understood. PCGF1/PRC1.1 selectively sustains astroglial lineage progression by repressing N4bp1 to stabilize Notch signaling.
Yin L, Zhou J, Ma Z, …, Peng X
▾

Abstract

The generation of distinct neural lineages from common progenitors requires coordinated regulation by developmental signaling pathways and epigenetic mechanisms. Although extracellular signals such as Notch promote astrocyte differentiation, how epigenetic mechanisms establish the competence of progenitors to respond to these cues remains poorly understood. Here, we identify PCGF1 (NSPC1), a core component of the non-canonical Polycomb repressive complex PRC1.1, as an epigenetic regulator of cortical astroglial lineage progression. Our findings reveal a PRC1.1-dependent mechanism that promotes astroglial lineage progression by sustaining developmental signaling output, establishing the PCGF1-N4BP1-Notch axis as a molecular link between chromatin regulation and neural lineage diversification.

EMBO Rep · IF 6.5 · 2026-10-09
Notch-hedgehog antagonism regulates stem cell niche formation through an E2-E3-E3 ubiquitin module.
Wang YT, Lai CM, Chen TA, …, Hsu HJ
▾

Abstract

Distinct microenvironments, including the stem cell niche and differentiation zone, promote stem cell self-renewal and progeny differentiation, yet how these domains are established remains unclear. In the developing Drosophila ovary, we show that Notch activation silences Hedgehog (Hh) signaling in anterior intermingled cells (ICs), specifying cap cells (CpCs) that form the germline stem cell (GSC) niche. Together, these findings identify a Notch-dependent E2-E3-E3 ubiquitin ligase module linking Notch activation to Hh silencing during niche patterning. This strategy may act broadly in cell-fate decisions and reveals a paradigm in which a developmental cue regulates an E2 enzyme to facilitate ubiquitin ligase assembly.

EMBO Rep · IF 6.5 · 2026-11-03
Self-organization of Drosophila chromatin architecture in a cell-free system.
Jayakrishnan M, Kars G, Campos-Sparr A, …, Becker PB
▾

Abstract

Metazoan genomes are organized by folding of the nucleosome fiber into loops and domains that support long-range regulatory interactions. Although cohesin-mediated loop extrusion and architectural DNA-binding proteins are central to current models of genome organization, how these mechanisms integrate to generate higher-order structure remains incompletely understood. Early Drosophila melanogaster embryogenesis provides a unique window into the emergence of chromatin architecture, as rapid syncytial nuclear divisions occur largely in the absence of transcription. However, probing the mechanisms underlying this primordial folding in vivo is technically challenging. Here, we establish an in vitro system that reconstitutes complex chromatin using extracts from syncytial embryos. Nucleosome mapping and Micro-C analyses reveal that long-range interactions, including cis and trans loops and topologically associating domains (TADs), emerge spontaneously from soluble extract components. While some structures resemble those observed in early embryos, others represent latent interaction potentials that are constrained in vivo. Focusing on the eve locus, we find that TAD formation is incompatible with a simple loop extrusion model and instead requires direct pairing of boundary elements mediated by the insulator protein 'Suppressor-of-hairy-wing' Su(Hw). Together, our work demonstrates that key features of 3D genome organization can be reconstituted in a cell-free system and provides a tractable platform for the mechanistic dissection of chromatin folding in Drosophila.

Intestinal FXR agonism synergizes with leptin to improve obesity by reducing appetite via secretin.
Zhu X, Wang K, Hu H, …, Wang H
▾

Abstract

Obesity is a chronic condition and is closely associated with various metabolic diseases. It has become a global health concern, and effective pharmacotherapies are urgently needed. Farnesoid X receptor (FXR) is widely regarded as a pivotal target for the treatment of metabolic diseases because of its many biological functions. To avoid the possible adverse side effects of systemic FXR agonism, intestinal-specific FXR agonists have been developed. They show excellent anti-obesity effects, but the underlying mechanism remains unclear. In this study, we demonstrate that intestinal-restricted FXR agonists exhibit marked anti-obesity effects in diet-induced obesity (DIO) and db/db mice, but not in ob/ob mice. Specifically, FXR agonism enhances histone H3K4 methylation at the Sct locus, while leptin per se suppresses DNA methylation at the same locus in a leptin-receptor independent manner. Collectively, the results of this study clarify the anti-obesity mechanism underlying intestinal FXR agonists.

Elife · IF 6.4 · 2026-10-20
Multiplexed CRISPRi reveals a transcriptional switch between KLF activators and repressors in the maturing neocortex.
Kirk RW, Sun L, Xiao R, …, Nelson SB
▾

Abstract

A critical phase of mammalian brain development takes place after birth. Neurons of the mouse neocortex undergo dramatic changes in their morphology, physiology, and synaptic connections during the first postnatal month, while properties of immature neurons, such as the capacity for robust axon outgrowth, are lost. The genetic and epigenetic programs controlling prenatal development are well studied, but our understanding of the transcriptional mechanisms that regulate postnatal neuronal maturation is comparatively lacking. By integrating chromatin accessibility and gene expression data from two subtypes of neocortical pyramidal neurons in the neonatal and maturing brain, we predicted a role for the Krüppel-like factor (KLF) family of transcription factors in the developmental regulation of neonatally expressed genes. Using a multiplexed CRISPR interference (CRISPRi) knockdown strategy, we found that a shift in expression from KLF activators (Klf6, Klf7) to repressors (Klf9, Klf13) during early postnatal development functions as a transcriptional 'switch' to first activate, then repress a set of shared targets with cytoskeletal functions including Tubb2b and Dpysl3. We demonstrate that this switch is buffered by redundancy between KLF paralogs, which our multiplexed CRISPRi strategy is equipped to overcome and study. Our results indicate that competition between activators and repressors within the KLF family regulates a conserved component of the postnatal maturation program that may underlie the loss of intrinsic axon growth in maturing neurons. This could facilitate the transition from axon growth to synaptic refinement required to stabilize mature circuits.

Genome Res · IF 6.2 · 2026-10-07
Shared transcriptional consequences of epigenetic machinery disruption in murine neuronal progenitors.
Ouyang J, Okay K, Möller K, …, Björnsson H
▾

Abstract

The Mendelian disorders of the epigenetic machinery (MDEMs) are an emerging group of disorders which commonly involve disruption of intellect and growth. To investigate consequences of epigenetic machinery (EM) disruption during neurodevelopment, we systematically knocked out (KO) EM factors in neuronal progenitors isolated from the murine hippocampus and established a neurodifferentiation model to interrogate their functions. We then profiled gene expression and DNA methylation (DNAm) in the EM-KOs using RNA sequencing and Nanopore long-read DNA sequencing. While Dnmt1-KO induces extensive DNAm alterations, Kmt2a-KO has little effect on methylation. Nevertheless, the disruption of Kmt2a and Dnmt1 produces convergent transcriptional changes. Loss of either EM factor leads to premature neuronal differentiation, partially explaining this convergence, and MYC emerges as a shared regulatory node linked to downregulation of cell cycle programs in these cells. Extending our methylation analysis to 46 EM genes, we find that loss of DNA methyltransferases induces the strongest DNAm changes, whereas other EM-KOs have subtle or negligible effects. However, clustering of EM-KOs based on promoter DNAm levels reveals three distinct EM subgroups, of which two are enriched for interactions with the DNAm machinery. Allele-specific analysis of DNAm further identifies a single differentially methylated region shared across the 46 EM-KOs, localized to the B6J allele over the Zic4 3'UTR. Furthermore, Zic4 overexpression appears to maintain the neuronal progenitor state, suggesting functional relevance of this locus. Taken together, our results reveal both gene-specific and convergent transcriptional effects across diverse EM-KOs and provide novel insights into the downstream consequences of EM dysfunction.

Temporal transcriptomics identifies trans-regulation by multiple lncRNAs in human iPSCs.
Liu M, Mamede I, Sofi S, …, Rinn JL
▾

Abstract

Some long noncoding RNAs (lncRNAs) are known to regulate gene expression. However, the underlying temporal dynamics of lncRNAs influencing gene and epigenetic regulation, as well as mechanisms of lncRNA regulation in trans, are less understood. To investigate this, we genetically engineer 17 doxycycline-inducible lncRNA transgenes for ectopic expression at the H11 safe harbor locus in human pluripotent stem cells (hiPSCs), and we generate high-density temporal RNA-seq and ATAC-seq profiles. Most lncRNA transgenes are induced at 2 h and maintain expression through the 96 h time course. Surprisingly, when we seek to identify gene expression changes owing to the lncRNAs, we find that the global transcriptional landscape is dominated by a strong systemic response triggered by doxycycline exposure. We rigorously define this cohort of genes as a doxycycline-responsive gene signature (DRGS). The DRGS is also present in at least 28 public data sets from dox-inducible transgene studies involving diverse cell types. Next, we determine which lncRNAs exhibit trans-regulatory events. We identify DANCR, FENDRR, LINC00667, LINC00847, LNCPRESS1, and LNCPNKY as lncRNAs that regulate specific transcript expression in trans The downstream target genes encode 53 mRNAs and 10 lncRNAs. None of the target lncRNAs alter gene expression proximal to their own loci (i.e., triggering secondary cis-effects). Surprisingly, the target genes of LINC00847 (transcribed from Chromosome 22) are substantially enriched on Chromosome 19, with a preponderance of target genes encoding RNA metabolism and RNA splicing factors. Collectively, our study provides a resource to discern artifacts in the doxycycline-inducible system and identifies temporally regulated targets of six lncRNAs for future mechanistic studies.

Genome Res · IF 6.2 · 2026-10-13
Parental genome reprogramming drives allelic convergence while preserving parent-of-origin memory during human early embryonic development.
Xu F, Liu Q, Du Z, …, Yan L
▾

Abstract

Human sperm and oocytes carry highly distinct DNA methylation landscapes, yet parental DNA methylation ultimately converges to a largely equivalent state, raising the question of when and how the allelic DNA methylome is reprogrammed toward convergence. Here, we present an integrated allelic analysis of DNA methylation and transcriptome dynamics throughout human pre- to peri-implantation development, a stage that lays the foundation for future development. We find that oocyte-specific hypermethylated regions retain maternal-biased DNA methylation asymmetry throughout early development, whereas paternal hypermethylation at sperm-specific regions is erased after fertilization and re-established during peri-implantation. Whole-genome parental DNA methylation levels converge across all lineages at embryonic days 8 to 12, prior to gastrulation, although the timing of this equilibrium differs among genomic elements. Notably, a subset of transposable elements, including SVAs and LTR12 elements, remains persistently maternally hypermethylated. Finally, we identify parent-of-origin-biased genes involved in early human embryonic development and find that these genes are enriched for dosage-sensitive genes. We further show that epiblast-specific parental expression bias is associated with genes implicated in neurodevelopmental disorders. These findings highlight the developmental and clinical relevance of allele-specific DNA methylation and transcription regulation during the earliest stages of human development.

Hum Reprod · IF 6.1 · 2026-10-30
Epididymis-derived RNAs in sperm affect the next generation: potential clinical implications.
Chen AJ, Meyers AJ, Rainsford S, …, Sakkas D
▾

Abstract

Until recently, the sole function of sperm was thought to be as delivery agents for the paternal contribution of zygote genes encoded in the DNA they carry. However, recent research has shown that sperm contribute substantially more information to oocytes upon fertilization through a select population of non-coding (nc)RNAs obtained while traversing the epididymis. Strikingly, their content can be altered by the environment in which males have lived, such as their exposure to various types of pollutants, traumas, addictive drugs, diet, and other factors. These changes in sperm ncRNA content can be responsible for transmitting the negative consequences of these environments to their offspring, via 'epigenetic inheritance', which is reminiscent of Lamarckian inheritance. This mini-review aims to provide an overview of the role of environment-regulated sperm ncRNAs in this process. We include studies on the relationship between specific paternal environments and the associated changes in sperm ncRNA content. These studies have been performed largely in rodent models, but we also describe evidence supporting the same processes occurring in humans. As an example, we focus on the epigenetic inheritance of stress phenotypes through changes in sperm miRNA profiles. We propose that identifying these specific ncRNAs carried by human sperm could lead to future diagnostic and treatment paradigms positively impacting ART. Screening out these potentially harmful sperm, or further research that provides approaches to reverse these changes before conception, may therefore prevent negative consequences related to epigenetic inheritance.

Abstract

BACKGROUND: Orofacial clefts, particularly cleft palate, are among the most common congenital anomalies, imposing substantial functional impairment and psychosocial burden on affected individuals. HSAT3 lncRNA regulates replication re-initiation and colocalizes with TOP2A under stress conditions.
Kanne J, Heß F, Min W, …, Schweiger MR
▾

Abstract

BACKGROUND: Long non-coding RNAs transcribed from pericentromeric Satellite 3 repeats (HSAT3) are involved in stress-dependent splicing processes and maintenance of genome stability. CONCLUSIONS: Together, our findings support a functional link between stress-induced repetitive RNAs, nuclear compartmentalization, and replication-associated processes. These results provide a framework for understanding how pericentromeric lncRNAs contribute to genome stability and drug response in cancer cells.

Kanne J, Heß F, Min W, Isensee J, Wasserburger-Zichel E, Tschernoster N, Dartenne L, Hoppe S, Altmüller J, Odenthal M, Hucho T, Grimm C, Schweiger MR[Journal Article]

Epigenetics Chromatin · IF 4.2 · 2026-10-22
Apparent methylation of mitochondrial DNA in WGBS is dominated by technical artifacts.
Yin JJ, Gong ZC, Mao SQ
▾

Abstract

BACKGROUND: DNA methylation is a critical epigenetic modification that occurs on cytosine residues and plays essential roles in regulating gene expression, cellular differentiation, and genomic imprinting. However, the existence of cytosine methylation in mitochondrial DNA (mtDNA) remains highly controversial. Whole-genome bisulfite sequencing (WGBS) has been widely used to assess mtDNA methylation, but signals detected in mitochondrial DNA are frequently influenced by technical artifacts. RESULTS: We systematically evaluated technical factors that influence mtDNA methylation measurements using zebrafish WGBS datasets derived from a wide range of tissues and experimental conditions, complemented by analyses of human and mouse datasets. mtDNA methylation measurements varied widely among samples within CpG, CHG, and CHH contexts, and mtDNA CpG methylation estimates above 2% were often accompanied by comparable CHG and CHH methylation estimates, even when lambda DNA CpG methylation remained below 2%. Notably, samples lacking documented DNA fragmentation tended to show substantially elevated mtDNA methylation compared with those subjected to fragmentation. Although strand-specific sequencing biases were common, they were not a sufficient cause of elevated false-positive methylation signals. The degree of strand coverage imbalance did not predict high methylation in datasets with documented fragmentation, and high methylation in undocumented samples was preferentially observed where strand coverage was balanced rather than imbalanced. Kit annotations were associated with dataset-level variation in mtDNA methylation, particularly for library preparation kits, but elevated signals were not confined to any single bisulfite conversion or library preparation kit. Cross-species analyses showed that human and mouse datasets displayed comparable patterns of elevated mtDNA methylation. CONCLUSIONS: Our findings indicate that high mtDNA methylation signals detected in WGBS data are dominated by technical artifacts, particularly DNA fragmentation status and bisulfite conversion efficiency. These results highlight the need for careful interpretation of mitochondrial methylation signals and provide a framework for distinguishing technical noise from potential biological signals in WGBS-based analyses.

Whole-genome 5-hydroxymethylation profiling of human neurons for regulatory and biological insight.
Klose D, Sepehri MH, Olsen RA, …, Jagodic M
▾

Abstract

While 5-methylcytosine (5mC) DNA methylation is a well-known player in genome stability and transcriptional regulation, the role of 5-hydroxymethylcytosine (5hmC), particularly prevalent in neurons, remains largely unknown. Here, we used long-read Oxford Nanopore Technology (ONT) to profile whole-genome, native 5mC and 5hmC levels in sorted neuronal nuclei samples from human post-mortem brain tissue. Altogether, our study demonstrates the potential of 5hmC and 5mC DNA modifications to provide biological insight into the regulatory landscape and function of human neurons.

Epigenetic maintenance of PRC2-repressed chromatin requires RTT109 but not H3K56 acetylation.

▾

Abstract

In animals, plants, and some fungi, Polycomb Repressive Complex 2 (PRC2) catalyzes trimethylation of histone H3 lysine 27 (H3K27me3) to establish transcriptionally repressed chromatin. Here, we identify the histone acetyltransferase RTT109 as a key regulator of PRC2-repressed domains in the model fungus Neurospora crassa. J Biol Chem · IF 4 · 2026-11-02
HDAC inhibition drives NOX5-dependent oxidative stress and sensitizes uveal melanoma cells to SOD1 inhibition.
Antony S, Devaiah BN, Konaté MM, …, Doroshow JH
▾

Abstract

Epigenetic dysregulation and reactive oxygen species (ROS) contribute to melanoma progression and therapeutic resistance, but their interplay in uveal melanoma (UM) remains incompletely understood. Analysis of TCGA data identified UM as expressing the highest levels of NADPH oxidase 5 (NOX5), a superoxide-producing enzyme, among human cancers. Consistent with a role for acetylation-dependent transcription, BRD4 knockdown or pharmacological inhibition attenuated NOX5 induction. However, the lack of a well-established selective NOX5 inhibitor presents a challenge for therapeutically exploiting NOX5 suppression. Conversely, combining Tacedinaline with the superoxide dismutase 1 (SOD1) inhibitor LCS1 significantly enhanced growth inhibition compared with either agent alone. These findings identify ROS modulation in either direction as a potential UM therapeutic strategy, while highlighting pharmacological ROS elevation through HDAC inhibition and disruption of antioxidant defenses as a potentially more practical approach to exploiting NOX5-driven oxidative vulnerability.

Abstract

Activating mutations in PIK3CA (encoding the p110α kinase) are among the most frequent oncogenic drivers in human tumors. Collectively, these findings demonstrate that the molecular network complexity of oncogenic PI3K can be interrogated through proximity labeling and that distinct PIK3CA point mutations rewire non-overlapping PPI networks, revealing domain-specific dependencies. Our study provides a framework for mapping mutation-specific interactomes and offers molecular insights and potential therapeutic strategies for PIK3CA-mutant tumors.

Genome-Wide DNA methylation profiling of tissue-plasma pairs identifies Novel cfDNA biomarkers for the diagnosis of gastric cancer.
Ou H, Miao J, Ling F, …, Qin Y
▾

Abstract

Screening for gastric cancer (GC) is critical for improving patient survival, yet current clinical methods suffer from invasiveness and limited sensitivity. The aim of this study is to develop a machine learning model for the non-invasive diagnosis of GC using the whole-genome methylation profile of circulating free DNA (cfDNA) in plasma. Plasma cfDNA from 61 patients with GC and 29 healthy controls. First, whole‑genome bisulfite sequencing (WGBS) was performed on 29 paired GC and adjacent normal tissues to identify differentially methylated regions (DMRs). These tissue DMRs were then mapped to plasma cfDNA methylation data, yielding 32 stable DMRs that changed consistently in both tissues and plasma. Using LASSO and Random Forest (RF) algorithms, eight core diagnostic markers were selected. Four machine learning algorithms (Support Vector Machine, RF, K-Nearest Neighbor, Naïve Bayes) were used to build diagnostic models. WGBS revealed global hypomethylation and CpG island hypermethylation in GC, consistent with plasma cfDNA methylation patterns. After adjusting for age, the model achieved an AUC of 0.924 on the test set, still demonstrating high discriminatory performance. Functional analysis implicated these markers in pathways such as phagocytosis and axon guidance, and showed significant correlation with immune cell infiltration. Notably, these core DMRs are not covered by the probes on the methylation arrays (450K and EPIC v2), highlighting the advantage of WGBS for novel biomarker discovery. In conclusion, tissue-plasma concordance analysis identified a panel of plasma cfDNA methylation biomarkers with strong diagnostic potential. The resulting RF model provides a promising non-invasive strategy for GC screening.

J Biol Chem · IF 4 · 2026-10-13
Differential histone tail citrullination by protein arginine deiminases observed via NMR spectroscopy.
Kowalczyk AJ, Morrison EA
▾

Abstract

Citrullination is a charge-modifying post-translational modification whereby proteinogenic arginine is converted to the non-coded amino acid citrulline by calcium-activated protein arginine deiminases (PADs; EC 3.5.3.15). The five known PAD enzymes in humans (PADs 1, 2, 3, 4, and 6) are differentially expressed and have distinct protein targets, including histones. While some PAD histone citrullination sites are known, a comprehensive investigation of all histone tail arginines targeted by catalytically active PADs 1-4 is lacking. Here, we sought to identify PAD citrullination sites in histone tails, within both histone peptides and reconstituted nucleosomes. Toward this objective, we utilized a real-time 1H-15N NMR spectroscopy-based assay. By monitoring both arginine and citrulline backbone amide peak intensities over time, we identified sites of citrullination in 15N-labeled histone tails within peptides and reconstituted nucleosome core particles. We found that PADs 1, 2, and 4 citrullinate all directly observable histone tail arginines to varying degrees. This is distinct from PAD3, which only moderately citrullinates H2A and H4 arginine residues and does not modify H3 tail arginines. Together, these data suggest a level of histone arginine specificity by each PAD. Furthermore, histone tail citrullination is altered within nucleosomes as compared to isolated peptides, which we interpret as reflecting differences in conformation and accessibility. We also speculate that citrullination increases nucleosomal histone tail dynamics, with implications for crosstalk between sites of histone citrullination and other important sites of regulation by PTMs (including lysines) within and between tails.

Development · IF 3.7 · 2026-10-26
Test of the DNMT1o imprinted maintenance methylation model reveals progressive methylation loss between 8-cell and blastocyst stages.
Matoushaya CN, Pena-Bello CA, Shaffer B, …, Mann MRW
▾

Abstract

DNMT1o, the maternal oocyte-derived isoform of DNA methyltransferase 1, is a key protein to maintain imprinted methylation. A model was proposed in 2001, and refined in 2008, where DNMT1o acts to maintain imprinted methylation exclusively at the 8-cell stage during preimplantation development. Nearly 20 years later, this is the first comprehensive analysis testing the maternal DNMT1o imprinted maintenance methylation model. We analyzed Snrpn and H19 imprinted methylation in individual control and maternal Dnmt1o-deficient mouse embryos across five preimplantation stages. Our findings challenged assumptions that DNMT1o acted solely at the 8-cell stage to produce ∼50% methylation loss in all embryos. Compared to controls, maternal Dnmt1o-deficient embryos experienced (1) progressive Snrpn and H19 imprinted methylation loss between the 8-cell to blastocyst stages, with inter-embryo variability extending beyond the predicted 50% loss; cleavage stage embryos exhibited similar methylation levels; (2) some embryos maintained maternal Snrpn and paternal H19 methylation, bypassing imprinted methylation loss; and (3) at the DNA strand level, Snrpn and H19 methylation loss was sporadic rather than complete. These findings point to potential compensation mechanisms during early preimplantation development.

SOX21 and SOX2 collaboratively promote blastocyst formation downstream of TFAP2C.
Aziz F, Savage K, Kim J, …, Ralston A

Abstract

In mammals, the first cell fate decisions segregate pluripotent fetal cells from the differentiating extraembryonic lineages. In mouse, pluripotent progenitors can be identified as early as the 16-cell (morula) stage, based on their restricted expression of SOX2. In spite of the unique SOX2 expression pattern, however, the first cell fate decision proceeds normally in the absence of Sox2, suggesting an undiscovered compensatory mechanism. We have discovered that the closely related SOX paralogue Sox21 is expressed alongside, and independently of, Sox2 prior to blastocyst formation. Mouse embryos lacking both Sox2 and Sox21 exhibit a phenotype more severe than those lacking either gene alone, arresting during the morula-to-blastocyst transition. In fact, embryos lacking any three copies of Sox2 or Sox21 frequently phenocopy double knockouts, consistent with a dose-dependent genetic interaction. Finally, we have discovered the mechanism promoting parallel expression of Sox2 and Sox21. We show that, like Sox2, expression of Sox21 depends on TFAP2C, and not NANOG or OCT4, clarifying gene regulatory mechanisms preceding blastocyst formation. Overall, our study reveals a new SOX-dependent pathway regulating embryonic processes even earlier than previously appreciated.

Abstract

Autologous transplantation of spermatogonial stem cells (SSCs) is a promising approach to restore fertility in young cancer patients who often become infertile due to gonadotoxic treatment. Microinsemination resulted in the production of allogeneic offspring. These results indicate that inhibition of the BTB by WIN18,446 is applicable in cases where endogenous SSCs prevent donor SSC colonization and further demonstrate the unique ability of cultured SSCs to colonize allogeneic testes.

Immune-related DNA methylation signatures, age prediction, and cross-tissue assessment via biologically informed neural networks.

Abstract

Immune-related DNA methylation patterns may provide interpretable markers of biological ageing and disease relevance. Prioritised features also showed cross-tissue consistency in infant buccal cells. Mendelian randomisation identified genetically supported associations of IL6 methylation with preterm birth and lower mean birth weight and of CXCR4 methylation with preterm birth after multiplicity correction. These associations do not establish that methylation itself causes the outcomes. Abstract

Trophoblast stem cells (TSCs) serve as a valuable system for investigating the signaling pathways that regulate extraembryonic lineage commitment. Abstract

PLoS One · IF 2.9 · 2026-10-07
Integrative methylome and transcriptome analyses identify LHX3 as an epigenetically downregulated marker with tumor suppressive properties in breast cancer.
Farooq K, Gull S, Farooq Z, …, Shahzad N
▾

Abstract

Breast cancer (BC) remains a major global health concern, with rising incidence and mortality rates. Early diagnosis of BC plays an important role in its prevention. However, number of clinically validated biomarkers that enable accurate and minimally invasive diagnosis are still insufficient. Epigenetic modifications, particularly hypermethylation-mediated inactivation of numerous tumor suppressor genes occur early in tumorigenesis and are detectable even before the clinical onset, making them promising candidates for early diagnosis. Therefore, this study aimed to identify breast cancer-associated epigenetically regulated genes with potential diagnostic and biological relevance. A set of forty genes that displayed both hypermethylation and downregulation in BC were initially identified through bioinformatics analysis of publicly available datasets. Based on Gene Ontology (GO) and Reactome pathway enrichment analyses, eight genes were subsequently shortlisted for experimental analysis. Among these eight genes, LHX3 was significantly downregulated in both MCF-7 and MDA-MB-231 cells and in all tested BC tissue samples. LHX3 promoter hypermethylation was found in 80% of tumor tissues, compared to 32% in matched normal tissues. Aberrant LHX3 promoter methylation was significantly associated with metastasis status (P = 0.0002), with higher methylation in non-metastatic tumors, tumor size ≤20 mm (P = 0.002), and advanced tumor stage (P = 0.005). Given the complex pattern of these associations, findings were interpreted cautiously. Treatment with 5-aza-2'-deoxycytidine reversed promoter hypermethylation and restored LHX3 expression in MDA-MB-231 and MCF-7 cells. Additionally, ectopic expression of LHX3 not only reduced migration and proliferation but also induced cell death in BC cells. Our study provided the first evidence of epigenetically induced silencing of LHX3 and its anti-tumor role in BC. Furthermore, our findings support LHX3 as a candidate epigenetically regulated biomarker with tumor-suppressive properties in BC; however, independent cohort validation, quantitative methylation assays, and stage/subtype-stratified analyses are required before its diagnostic or prognostic utility can be established.

Optimised fluorescence-activated nuclei sorting for epigenomic analysis of cortical cell types.
Chioza B, Policicchio S, Burrage J, …, Dempster EL
▾

Abstract

Increased understanding of the functional complexity of the genome has led to growing recognition of the role of non-sequence-based regulatory variation in disorders of the human central nervous system. Most genomic analyses of the brain are limited by the use of bulk tissue, which comprises a heterogeneous mix of different neural cell types with distinct epigenetic profiles, thereby limiting the ability to attribute regulatory changes to specific cell populations. Given the limited availability of human post-mortem tissue resources and the importance of integrating multi-omic data from the same samples, there is a critical need for methods that enable parallel, cell-type-resolved genomic profiling. We present optimised protocols using fluorescence-activated nuclei sorting (FANS) to isolate nuclei from different human and mouse brain cell types for downstream multi-omic analysis. Our approach enables the robust purification of neuronal, oligodendrocyte, microglial and other glial-origin nuclei from both adult and fetal brain tissue. We demonstrate that FANS-isolated nuclei are compatible with a wide range of genomic assays, including profiling of DNA modifications, histone modifications, chromatin accessibility, and gene expression. This protocol maximises the utility of limited post-mortem tissue resources and provides a unified workflow for comprehensive, cell-type-specific interrogation of molecular mechanisms involved in the brain.

▾

Abstract

Accurate quantification of DNA methylation at specific CpG sites is essential for epigenetic studies, yet measurements can vary depending on assay design and experimental context. Here, we examined locus-specific DNA methylation quantification across three widely used bisulfite-based assays, Illumina Infinium MethylationEPIC array, QIAseq targeted methyl sequencing (TMS), and pyrosequencing, using identical DNA samples. This controlled framework enabled evaluation of method-dependent measurement behavior across CpG sites and a range of methylation levels while minimizing biological variability. Across shared CpGs, all three platforms consistently captured relative methylation trends; however, absolute methylation values differed systematically in a CpG- and methylation-dependent manner. Pyrosequencing generally reported higher methylation levels compared with TMS, whereas EPIC array measurements showed increased variability at low methylation levels (<10%), resulting in reduced inter-method agreement in this range. In contrast, concordance between methods improved at moderate-to-high methylation levels. Error estimates and agreement analyses further indicated that measurement variability was not uniform across CpGs, highlighting site-specific and methylation-level-dependent effects. Together, these results demonstrate that locus-specific DNA methylation quantification using bisulfite-based assays is influenced by local CpG context and methylation level. While relative methylation patterns are preserved across platforms, absolute values are not directly interchangeable, particularly at low methylation levels. These findings underscore the importance of context-aware interpretation and orthogonal validation when precise, locus-specific DNA methylation quantification is required.

PLoS One · IF 2.9 · 2026-10-14
Personalized genomic landscape of prostate cancer in African American men reveals novel drivers and therapeutic opportunities.
Murthy S, Fago K, Knight J, …, Jung M
▾

Abstract

African American (AA) men experience disproportionately high prostate cancer incidence and mortality. Despite this disparity, current genomic repositories are predominantly derived from European populations, and studies using archived tissues are often limited by stromal contamination. We sought to characterize the high-resolution somatic mutational landscape of AA prostate cancer using a high-fidelity, patient-matched model system to identify population-specific drivers and therapeutic targets. We performed whole-genome sequencing (WGS) on 10 matched pairs of primary cell cultures derived from prostate tumors and adjacent non-malignant tissues of AA patients. Our analyses revealed a complex, heterogeneous landscape characterized by significant genomic instability, including frequent chromothripsis and kataegis signatures. Mutation hotspots were concentrated on chromosomes 1, 2, 3, and X, with the highest mutation density on chromosome 17. Functionally impactful mutations were identified in critical oncogenic pathways, including chromatin remodeling (CHD1, ARID1A, ATRX, KDM6A) and DNA damage response (ATM, BRCA1/2, RAD51). Additionally, we identified potentially novel candidate mutations unique to this AA cohort, distinct from those in European-centric datasets. This study provides a foundational, patient-specific WGS dataset derived from African American prostate cancer cell models. The identification of high-frequency actionable alterations in epigenetic and DNA repair pathways highlights the utility of this renewable resource for developing tailored therapeutic interventions and addressing racial disparities in prostate cancer outcomes.

PLoS One · IF 2.9 · 2026-10-09
Transcriptomic analysis of zebrafish (Danio rerio) liver exposed to sediments from the Loa River.
Lobos-Parra N, Leiva NV, Larama G, …, Orrego R
▾

Abstract

Metal(loid) contamination in aquatic ecosystems, particularly the Loa River in Chile, poses significant environmental and health challenges. This study investigated the molecular impacts of such contamination by exposing zebrafish (Danio rerio) to sediments from pre-impact and severely impacted zones of the Loa River, utilizing transcriptomic analysis of zebrafish liver. Sediment characterization confirmed arsenic as the main driver of contamination, with As concentrations significantly higher at La Finca. Our findings reveal a clear and distinct cascade of molecular responses in fish exposed to impacted sediments, which provides evidence for an Adverse Outcome Pathway. This AOP commences with Molecular Initiating Events, including significant differential expression of genes related to metal adsorption and homeostasis (slc31a1, slc40a1; FDR-adjusted q < 0.05; p < 0.05 for IT7 vs C0 and IT7 vs PIT7), signifying direct perturbations in intracellular metal regulation. This is followed by Key Events characterized by heightened cellular stress, evidenced by significant upregulation of xbp1, xbp1a, xbp1b, and hsp90a/hspd1 (IT7 vs C0; p < 0.05), consistent with oxidative stress and endoplasmic reticulum dysfunction, as well as hypoxia-like conditions. Progressing further, significant differential expression of angiogenesis-related genes (ptprb, mib1, adam10a, foxc1a; p < 0.05) suggests activation of tissue remodeling processes. The cascade culminates in alterations of genes associated with early carcinogenic signaling, including epigenetic regulation (hdac1) and genomic instability (rassf1, top2b; FDR q < 0.05; p < 0.05). This research demonstrates the power of transcriptomics to differentiate the biological impact of contaminated sediments from pre-impact conditions, offering a detailed molecular fingerprint of sublethal effects crucial for identifying early warning signals of environmental degradation and understanding potential human health impacts.

Nepton A, Faber SC, Kaminsky Z, …, Perez Rosal SR
▾

Abstract

Ketamine produces rapid antidepressant effects, but the molecular processes associated with their persistence remain poorly defined in humans. We examined longitudinal DNA methylation changes in 10 adults with major depressive disorder undergoing ketamine-assisted psychotherapy using two pre-treatment assessments (T1, T2) and one post-treatment assessment (T3). Participants received three sessions of intranasal racemic ketamine within a structured psychotherapy protocol. Buccal DNA methylation was measured using the Illumina MethylationEPIC v2.0 BeadChip. Depressive symptoms improved markedly following treatment, with mean Beck Depression Inventory-II scores declining from the severe to the mild range (p < .001). Of 802,595 CpG loci evaluated, 20,770 met the nominal T3-versus-T2 screening criterion. Sequential filtering for methylation-change magnitude and movement relative to pre-treatment fluctuation identified 962 retained CpG loci, including 517 retained gene-annotated loci. A supplementary linear mixed-effects analysis modeled repeated measurements within participants across the 603 preselected gene-annotated magnitude-filtered loci; because these loci were selected using the same contrast and cohort, this analysis was not treated as independent confirmation. Exploratory enrichment analysis of this 603-locus set identified NOTCH1-related Reactome terms driven by NOTCH1, DLL1, and ADAM17. Within the retained signature, multiple core and adjacent Notch-related components formed a coherent signaling module spanning ligand, receptor processing, transcriptional regulation, and pathway cross-talk. These findings identify a structured peripheral methylation signature associated with ketamine-assisted psychotherapy. They do not establish brain-specific pathway activation or causality, but provide a longitudinal basis for investigating regulatory adaptations accompanying clinical improvement.

Kao SH, Singh LN, Starr J, …, Kilbaugh TJ
▾

Abstract

The human brain consists of diverse cell types that collaboratively perform complex functions and form intricate neuronal networks. When traumatic brain injury (TBI) occurs, multiple molecular pathways involving diverse cell types are activated in response to injury. Understanding how brain cell composition is altered during TBI may help improve patient outcomes. Upon brain trauma, neurovascular disruption leads to the release of brain-derived cell-free DNA (cfDNA) into peripheral blood. The tissue-specific DNA methylation patterns in this cfDNA have the potential to serve as candidate markers for studying brain injury. These analyses identified candidate brain cell type-enriched cfDNA methylation features that differed across post-injury sampling groups. Together, our findings highlight that brain cell type-enriched methylation signatures may provide a powerful tool for characterizing distinct cell populations and nominating candidate brain cell-associated cfDNA methylation biomarkers in TBI.

Biol Open · IF 2.3 · 2026-10-12
Nuclear blebs are composed of variable chromatin states and display increased transcription initiation relative to elongation.
Clark M, Losada A, Jahng S, …, Stephens AD
▾

Abstract

Nuclear blebs are herniations of the nucleus that occur in many human conditions including aging, heart disease, muscular dystrophy, and many cancers. Nuclear blebbing causes nuclear rupture and cellular dysfunction. However, understanding the formation, stability, and identification of nuclear blebs remains an ongoing challenge. Our previous studies revealed that nuclear blebs are best hallmarked by decreased DNA density. To determine if chromatin decompaction underlies decreased DNA density in nuclear blebs, we investigated the histone composition of nuclear blebs across multiple cell lines. Time-lapse and immunofluorescence imaging revealed that histone levels decreased in the nuclear bleb compared to the nuclear body but were more variable than DNA. Next, we imaged histone modification states of euchromatin and heterochromatin, which track the decondensed and compact states of chromatin, respectively. We found that nuclear blebs displayed variable histone modification states across cell lines, as euchromatin is not consistently enriched nor is heterochromatin consistently depleted. Nuclear blebs did consistently show that active RNA polymerase II initiation is significantly increased relative to elongation. Thus, decreased DNA density that hallmarks nuclear blebs is not due to local histone modification state, and the increase in transcription initiation relative to elongation is reproducible in nuclear blebs.

bioRxiv · 2026-10-25
Adenosylhomocysteinase drives epigenetic and metabolic reprogramming in ZFTA-RELA ependymoma.
Yadav N, Pelcher O, Mooli RGR, …, Michealraj KA
▾

Abstract

UNLABELLED: Supratentorial ependymomas (ST-EPNs) are aggressive pediatric brain tumors predominantly driven by the recurrent ZFTA-RELA fusion, an oncogenic chromatin regulator that establishes an aberrant transcriptional program. Although metabolic pathways modulate epigenetic regulation by controlling the availability of metabolic cofactors, how ZFTA-RELA coordinates metabolic reprogramming with epigenetic regulation is not well understood. Here, we show that ZFTA-RELA-positive ependymomas exhibit enhanced methionine dependency, elevated adenosyl homocysteinase (AHCY) expression, and epigenetically control aspartate metabolism for tumor growth. Mechanistically, ZFTA-RELA upregulates AHCY, increasing cellular methylation potential which promotes H3K4me3-dependent expression of CAD, the first enzyme complex of de novo pyrimidine biosynthesis, while simultaneously enforcing H3K27me3 mediated repression of ASNS, which encodes asparagine synthetase. Through this coordinated epigenetic program, aspartate utilization is directed toward pyrimidine biosynthesis while endogenous asparagine production is limited. Restricting S-adenosylmethionine availability or genetically or pharmacologically inhibiting AHCY reverses these epigenetic states, leading to CAD downregulation, ASNS upregulation, impaired nucleotide synthesis, and suppression of tumor growth in vitro and in vivo. Similarly, EZH2 inhibition also restores ASNS expression and suppresses tumor proliferation. These findings suggest that ZFTA-RELA EPN maintain ASNS-low phenotype which conditionally make them dependent on extracellular asparagine. We further show that enhanced de novo pyrimidine synthesis not only supports nucleotide production but also provides UDP-sugars required for N-glycosylation and stabilization of the asparagine transporter SLC1A5, thereby promoting extracellular asparagine uptake and tumor proliferation. Collectively, our findings identify AHCY as a critical metabolic regulator of epigenetic and metabolic reprogramming that drives pyrimidine synthesis and establishes extracellular asparagine dependency, revealing actionable metabolic vulnerabilities in ZFTA-RELA-positive ST-EPNs. Paternal regulation of H3K4 methylation supports tumor suppressor networks in mammals intergenerationally.
Walters BW, Heuer RA, Yu H, …, Lesch BJ
▾

Abstract

Paternally-inherited epigenetic information can influence phenotype in offspring (1). Here, we identify a critical mechanistic contribution of KDM6A (UTX), an X-linked histone modifier and tumor suppressor, in regulating transmissible epigenetic information in mammalian sperm. Paternal loss of KDM6A increases cancer risk in genetically wild type offspring, but how Kdm6a knockout sperm transmit this effect at the molecular level is unknown (2). We find that KDM6A functions in spermatogenesis to promote methylation of histone H3 lysine 4 (H3K4) via selective interaction with the COMPASS complex methyltransferase KMT2C (MLL3). KMT2C and KDM6A are coordinately recruited to promoters of active genes in spermatogenic cells, contrasting with recruitment to intergenic enhancers in other cell types (3, 4). Loss of KDM6A disrupts H3K4 methylation at promoters of tumor suppressor genes in spermatogonia, and some of these defects persist in epididymal sperm and correspond to impaired expression in preimplantation embryos. These genes are also misregulated in normal and malignant hematopoietic tissue of genetically wild type offspring, indicating that impaired H3K4 methylation in KDM6A-deficient male germ cells may preferentially alter regulation of tumor suppressor gene networks in development across generations.

bioRxiv · 2026-10-11
Prion-induced heritable states in Caenorhabditis elegans.
Ilbay O, Zabinsky RA, Lozanoski T, …, Fire A
▾

Abstract

Self-replicating protein conformations can encode persistent epigenetic states that underlie prion diseases and contribute to age-related neurodegenerative disorders in mammals. In fungi, such conformers are transmitted through mitosis and meiosis, enabling robust inheritance of diverse phenotypes. Whether analogous transgenerational protein-based inheritance extends beyond unicellular systems to metazoans remains unknown. Here, we address this question in C. elegans, a genetically tractable, short-lived animal well suited for tracking the transmission of heritable information across generations. We found that a yeast prion domain, when fused to a host protein, induced a heritable altered state that arose at elevated temperatures and persisted across generations at lower temperatures. The emergence of this state depended on known prionogenic yeast repeats, while the state itself was reversible and behaved semi-dominantly in crosses, consistent with a protein-based epigenetic mechanism. These findings provide direct evidence that protein domains capable of adopting self-replicating conformers can encode heritable information in an animal.

bioRxiv · 2026-10-25
Adenosine refines the balance of spermatogonial proliferation and differentiation.
Wang P, Nguyen D, Xiao C, …, Wang Y
▾

Abstract

The balance between spermatogonial proliferation and differentiation is essential for sustaining male fertility, yet the contribution of cellular metabolism remains poorly understood. Here, we showed that adenosine levels increased significantly during spermatogonial differentiation and promoted the formation of differentiating spermatogonia. Using a conditional knockout mouse model in which Ahcy, a gene encoding an enzyme in adenosine synthesis, is deleted from spermatogonia, we found that disruption of adenosine generation blocked spermatogonial differentiation, resulting in a transient delay in spermatogenesis. Taken together, our study identified adenosine as a metabolic signal in refining the balance between spermatogonial proliferation and differentiation, linking cellular metabolism to critical developmental timing and stem cell fate decisions.

bioRxiv · 2026-10-11
Disease-associated mutations directionally modulate epigenetic age in iPSC-derived tissues.
Skvir NJ, Dowrey TW, Cranston SF, …, Murphy GJ
▾

Abstract

Reprogramming somatic cells into induced pluripotent stem cells (iPSCs) resets epigenetic age, creating a major challenge for modeling age-associated cellular states and limiting the use of iPSCs in aging research beyond the genetic component. Recent work has shown that healthy organoids can re-accumulate age-associated epigenetic states over extended culture; whether disease-associated genetic states can directionally reshape these trajectories after reprogramming remains unknown. Here, we present a reproducible, in-vitro approach to re-establish epigenetic aging trajectories using iPSC-derived organoid systems, as quantified by multiple epigenetic clock models. We first demonstrate that epigenetic age progressively increases with serial passaging in a lung organoid model (alveolospheres). We then show that disease-associated mutations in multiple models drive additional, cell-intrinsic shifts in epigenetic age, implicating disease state as an independent modulator of epigenetic clocks. Together, these findings reinforce that iPSC-derived organoids recapitulate epigenetic aging trajectories over time, while highlighting disease state as a potent and potentially tractable modulator of epigenetic age in vitro. By enabling more faithful modeling of age-associated cellular states, this approach expands the utility of iPSC-based systems for dissecting the molecular mechanisms that regulate human aging.
